## 📊 四、实验结果

### 数据集

| 数据集 | 实体数 | 关系数 | 学习者 | 课程数 |
|--------|--------|--------|--------|--------|
| Xuetang | 5,101 | 106,468 | 6,548 | 687 |
| COCO | 4,378 | 51,210 | 25,979 | 23,319 |

### 主要性能对比（Table 1，K=10）

| 方法 | Xuetang NDCG | Xuetang HR | COCO NDCG | COCO HR |
|------|-------------|-----------|----------|---------|
| NeuMF（协同过滤） | 14.05 | 39.02 | 5.55 | 16.45 |
| MAECR（最强神经网络） | 21.98 | 49.08 | 9.56 | 25.06 |
| UPGPR（路径方法） | 20.85 | 47.78 | 8.88 | 23.77 |
| **KnowPath** | **23.55** | **55.68** | **10.52** | **29.68** |

> **KnowPath比最强神经网络基线（MAECR）提升7.3% NDCG，比最强路径方法（UPGPR）提升13%**

### 可解释性评估（Table 3）

| 模型 | Fidelity(5%) | Fidelity(10%) | Sparsity(3跳) | Sparsity(5跳) |
|------|-------------|--------------|-------------|-------------|
| UPGPR | 0.73 | 0.67 | 0.92 | 0.85 |
| **KnowPath** | **0.90** | **0.86** | **0.99** | **0.95** |

> **Fidelity越高**：解释越忠实于模型决策
> **Sparsity越高**：解释越简洁聚焦

### 啰嗦偏差消除验证（Table 5）

| 数据集 | 条件 | Spearman ρ | 结论 |
|--------|------|-----------|------|
| Xuetang | 无惩罚 | 0.59 | 严重偏差 |
| Xuetang | **有惩罚** | **0.03** | **偏差消除** |
| COCO | 无惩罚 | 0.56 | 严重偏差 |
| COCO | **有惩罚** | **0.02** | **偏差消除** |

```
（解决语义歧义）               GPT-4o提取Topic+摘要

三步KG构建流程           →    你的知识图谱构建Pipeline
（分块→提取→摘要）            完全可以参考这个流程

多维度奖励模型           →    你的Evaluation Agent
（有用性+准确性-啰嗦）         多维度评估学习效果

路径即解释               →    你的Inline Coach Panel
（KG路径→自然语言）            推荐理由的可解释输出

MoE动态权重             →    你的Adaptation Agent
（情境自适应）                 根据学生情况动态调整
```

### 你可以直接借鉴的三个具体设计

**① 实体摘要解决语义歧义**

你的系统也面临类似问题：
```
用户说"我想学机器学习"
  → 有很多不同深度的"机器学习"课程
  → 需要区分：入门版 vs 进阶版 vs 研究版

借鉴KnowPath：
  为每个知识点生成LLM摘要
  → 摘要包含：难度、适合人群、前置要求
  → 帮助Planning Agent精准匹配用户水平
```

**② 多维度评估框架**

```python
class EvaluationAgent:
    """
    借鉴KnowPath的多维度奖励思想
    不只看分数，还看多个维度
    """
    
    def evaluate_learning_session(self, quiz_result, content):
        
        # 维度1：知识掌握度（最大化）
        mastery_score = quiz_result.score / 100
        
        # 维度2：内容相关性（最大化）
        # 内容是否和学习目标相关
        relevance = self.check_relevance(
            content, user_profile.goal
        )
        
        # 维度3：内容复杂度（需要平衡）
        # 借鉴KnowPath的啰嗦惩罚思想
        # 内容太简单或太复杂都不好
        complexity_match = self.check_complexity(
            content, user_profile.level
        )
        
        # 综合EP（借鉴CSEAL）
        ep = (quiz_result.score - quiz_result.score_before) / \
             (100 - quiz_result.score_before)
        
        return {
            'ep': ep,
            'mastery': mastery_score,
            'relevance': relevance,
            'complexity_match': complexity_match
        }
```

**③ 路径可解释性输出**

```python
class InlineCoachPanel:
    """
    借鉴KnowPath的路径即解释思想
    把知识图谱路径转化为自然语言解释
    """
    
    def explain_recommendation(self, kg_path, user_profile):
        """
        把知识图谱路径转化为自然语言推荐理由
        """
        # 例：知识图谱路径
        # 用户 → [需要先学] → 线性代数
        #      → [是先修知识] → 梯度下降
        #      → [是目标] → 神经网络
        
        prompt = f"""
        根据以下知识图谱路径，
        为学生生成一句简洁的推荐理由：
        
        路径：{kg_path}
        学生水平：{user_profile.level}
        
        要求：
        1. 一句话，不超过30字
        2. 说明为什么推荐这个内容
        3. 不要说"根据你的..."等明显标签化的话
        """
        
        explanation = self.llm.call(prompt)
        return explanation
```

# 纯中文版（给毕设老师讲）

---

## KnowPath论文解读与毕设应用分析

### 一、论文概述

本论文由浙江师范大学朱佳等人发表于ACM Transactions on Information Systems 2026年1月，是信息系统领域的顶级期刊成果。论文提出了KnowPath框架，将大语言模型（LLM）与强化学习（RL）结合，用于MOOC平台的可解释课程推荐。其核心创新在于：用LLM生成实体摘要解决同名课程的语义歧义问题，并设计了包含负向维度的多目标奖励模型，使推荐结果既准确又可解释。

### 二、核心问题与解决方案

**现有方法的两大缺陷：**

第一，传统知识图谱构建成本极高，且在MOOC场景中面临严重的语义歧义问题。例如，同一平台上可能同时存在多门名为"计算机科学导论"的课程，但内容、深度和适用人群完全不同，传统三元组方式无法有效区分。

第二，现有强化学习推荐方法使用一维奖励函数，无法同时考虑有用性、准确性、简洁性等多个相互制约的维度。

**KnowPath的两阶段解决方案：**

**第一阶段：LLM构建知识图谱**

采用三步流程：首先用TextTiling算法自适应分块（上限800 tokens）；然后用LLM提取实体和关系，通过迭代追问确保完整性；最后为每个实体生成LLM摘要，捕捉隐含关系，解决语义歧义。与传统三元组相比，每个实体都附有丰富的摘要描述，表达能力更强。

**第二阶段：RL路径推荐**

将推荐过程建模为MDP，状态为当前实体嵌入加历史选课嵌入：

$$s_t = (\mathbf{v}_t, \{h_1, ..., h_m\}, p_{1:t})$$

奖励函数包含三个分量：

$$r_t = \alpha_1 \mathbf{w}^\top f_\theta(x \oplus y) + \alpha_2 \mathbb{I}(p_{1:t} \in \mathcal{P}_{valid}) + \alpha_3 \mathbf{v}_l^\top \mathbf{v}_c$$

其中任务奖励为多维度（有用性正向、啰嗦负向），路径合法性确保推荐有教育意义，语义相似度确保推荐符合学习者偏好。使用PPO算法优化策略，并引入MoE门控网络动态调整各维度权重。

推荐结果以知识图谱路径的形式呈现，路径本身即为解释。例如5跳路径"学习者→选课→课程X→教授→教师Y→教授→课程C"可解释为"你选的课程的老师还教了课程C"。

### 三、实验结果

在Xuetang和COCO两个公开MOOC数据集上，KnowPath的NDCG@10分别达到23.55和10.52，比最强神经网络基线提升7.3%，比最强路径方法提升13%。可解释性评估显示Fidelity达到0.90（对比基线0.73），Sparsity达到0.99（对比基线0.92）。啰嗦偏差消除实验证明，加入惩罚项后响应长度与奖励分数的相关性从0.59降至0.03，有效消除了偏差。

### 四、对本毕设的应用价值

**三步KG构建流程的直接借鉴：**

KnowPath的三步流程（分块→提取→摘要）与本系统的知识图谱构建方案高度吻合。特别是"实体摘要"这一步，可以为本系统的每个知识点生成包含难度、适用人群、前置要求的摘要，帮助Planning Agent更精准地匹配用户水平，避免推荐内容过难或过易。

**多维度评估框架的启示：**

KnowPath的多目标奖励模型启示本系统的Evaluation Agent不应只看测验分数，还应综合考虑内容相关性、难度匹配度等多个维度，形成更全面的学习效果评估。

**路径可解释性的设计思路：**

KnowPath将知识图谱路径直接作为推荐解释，这与本系统Inline Coach Panel的设计理念一致。本系统可以借鉴这一思路，将知识图谱中的先修关系和相似关系路径转化为自然语言解释，为每个推荐内容提供清晰的理由。

**写作引用建议：**

> "本系统的知识图谱构建方案参考了KnowPath（Zhu等，2026）提出的三步流程，通过LLM为每个知识概念生成丰富的摘要描述，有效解决了同名知识点的语义歧义问题，并提升了知识图谱的表达能力。"

# Pure English Version (For Thesis Supervisor)

## Analysis of KnowPath and Its Application to This Thesis Project

### I. Paper Overview

This paper, published in ACM Transactions on Information Systems in January 2026 by Zhu et al. from Zhejiang Normal University, proposes KnowPath — a framework combining LLMs and Reinforcement Learning for explainable MOOC course recommendations. Its core innovations are: using LLM-generated entity summaries to resolve semantic ambiguities in same-name courses, and designing a multi-objective reward model with negative dimensions to produce accurate yet explainable recommendations.

### II. Core Problems and Solutions

**Two fundamental flaws in existing methods:**

First, traditional KG construction is costly and faces severe semantic ambiguity in MOOC settings. For example, a platform may offer multiple courses all titled "Introduction to Computer Science" but with completely different content, depth, and target audiences — a distinction that traditional triple-based KG methods cannot effectively capture.

Second, existing RL recommendation methods use one-dimensional reward functions, unable to simultaneously balance multiple competing objectives such as helpfulness, correctness, and conciseness.

**KnowPath's two-stage solution:**

**Stage 1: LLM-based KG Construction**

A three-step pipeline: adaptive chunking with TextTiling (800-token upper bound); LLM extraction of entities and relationships with iterative completeness checking; and LLM-generated summaries for each entity to capture implicit relationships and resolve ambiguities. Unlike traditional triples, each entity is enriched with descriptive summaries, yielding greater expressiveness.

**Stage 2: RL Path-Based Recommendation**

The recommendation process is modeled as an MDP with state:

$$s_t = (\mathbf{v}_t, \{h_1, ..., h_m\}, p_{1:t})$$

The reward function contains three components:

$$r_t = \alpha_1 \mathbf{w}^\top f_\theta(x \oplus y) + \alpha_2 \mathbb{I}(p_{1:t} \in \mathcal{P}_{valid}) + \alpha_3 \mathbf{v}_l^\top \mathbf{v}_c$$

where the task reward is multi-dimensional (helpfulness positive, verbosity negative), path validity ensures educational meaningfulness, and semantic similarity ensures alignment with learner preferences. PPO optimizes the policy, and a MoE gating network dynamically adjusts dimension weights by context.

Recommendations are presented as KG paths that serve directly as explanations. For example, a 5-hop path "Learner → enrolled → Course X → teaches → Teacher Y → teaches → Course C" is explained as "The teacher of a course you enrolled in also teaches Course C."

### III. Experimental Results

On Xuetang and COCO datasets, KnowPath achieves NDCG@10 of 23.55 and 10.52 respectively, outperforming the strongest neural baseline by 7.3% and the strongest path-based method by 13%. Explainability evaluation shows Fidelity of 0.90 (vs. baseline 0.73) and Sparsity of 0.99 (vs. baseline 0.92). The verbosity bias elimination experiment demonstrates that the penalty term reduces the Spearman correlation between response length and reward score from 0.59 to 0.03.

### IV. Application to This Thesis Project

**Direct adoption of the three-step KG construction pipeline:**

KnowPath's three-step pipeline (chunking → extraction → summarization) closely aligns with this system's knowledge graph construction approach. The "entity summarization" step in particular can be applied to generate summaries for each knowledge concept containing difficulty level, target audience, and prerequisites, helping the Planning Agent more precisely match content to the user's level.

**Multi-dimensional evaluation framework:**

KnowPath's multi-objective reward model suggests that this system's Evaluation Agent should not rely solely on quiz scores, but should also consider content relevance and difficulty alignment to form a more comprehensive assessment of learning effectiveness.

**Path-based explainability design:**

KnowPath's use of KG paths as direct explanations aligns with this system's Inline Coach Panel design philosophy. This system can adopt a similar approach, converting prerequisite and similarity relationships from the knowledge graph into natural language explanations for each recommended content item.

**Recommended citation in thesis:**

> "The knowledge graph construction approach of this system references the three-step pipeline proposed by KnowPath (Zhu et al., 2026), using LLM-generated summaries to enrich each knowledge concept with descriptive information, effectively resolving semantic ambiguities among same-name concepts and enhancing the expressiveness of the knowledge graph."

## 一、你现在的Pipeline vs KnowPath的三步流程

```
你现在的Pipeline：
════════════════════════════════════════
文档输入
  ↓
LLM抽取知识点（Topic/SubTopic）
  ↓
KeyBERT + Sentence-BERT → similarity边
  ↓
LLM判断先修关系 → prerequisite边
  ↓
存入NetworkX图

KnowPath的三步：
════════════════════════════════════════
Step 1：分块（TextTiling自适应分块）
Step 2：提取实体+关系（LLM）
Step 3：生成实体摘要（LLM）← 你缺这步！

对比：
  Step 1：你有（文档输入）
  Step 2：你有（LLM抽取+关系构建）
  Step 3：你没有！← 这就是要结合的地方
```

```
                               和Softmax函数",

                       "Scaled Dot-Product"]
```

## 四、具体代码：加在哪里，怎么加

```python
import networkx as nx
from keybert import KeyBERT
from sentence_transformers import SentenceTransformer
import numpy as np
import json

# ══════════════════════════════════════════════
# 你原有的步骤（保持不变）
# ══════════════════════════════════════════════

def extract_topics_from_doc(document, llm):
    """Step 1+2：你原有的抽取逻辑"""
    prompt = f"""
    从以下文档中提取知识点：
    {document}
    
    输出JSON格式：
    {{
      "topics": [
        {{
          "name": "知识点名称",
          "sub_topics": ["子概念1", "子概念2"]
        }}
      ]
    }}
    """
    return llm.call(prompt)

def build_similarity_edges(topics, threshold=0.75):
    """你原有的相似度计算"""
    kw_model = KeyBERT()
    sbert = SentenceTransformer(
        'paraphrase-multilingual-MiniLM-L12-v2'
    )
    # ... 你原有的代码 ...

def build_prerequisite_edges(topics, llm):
    """你原有的先修关系判断"""
    # ... 你原有的代码 ...


# ══════════════════════════════════════════════
# 新增：KnowPath的实体摘要步骤
# ══════════════════════════════════════════════

def generate_entity_summaries(topics, llm, user_profile=None):
    """
    借鉴KnowPath的Step 3：为每个知识点生成摘要
    
    这一步的作用：
    1. 帮助Planning Agent判断难度是否匹配用户
    2. 帮助Content Agent生成个性化讲义
    3. 解决同名知识点的语义歧义
    """
    
    summaries = {}
    
    for topic in topics:
        
        # 构建摘要生成Prompt
        prompt = f"""
        你是一位教育专家。请为以下知识点生成结构化摘要。
        
        知识点：{topic['name']}
        子概念：{topic.get('sub_topics', [])}
        
        请生成JSON格式的摘要：
        {{
          "description": "用2-3句话描述这个知识点是什么",
          "difficulty": <1-5的整数，1最简单5最难>,
          "target_audience": "适合什么水平的学习者",
          "prerequisites_detail": "学习前需要掌握什么",
          "learning_time": "预计学习时间（分钟）",
          "key_concepts": ["核心概念1", "核心概念2"],
          "common_misconceptions": "学习者常见的误解",
          "real_world_applications": "实际应用场景"
        }}
        
        注意：difficulty的标准
          1 = 高中生可以理解
          2 = 大学本科基础
          3 = 大学本科进阶
          4 = 研究生水平
          5 = 专家级别
        """
        
        summary = llm.call(prompt)
        
        # 解析JSON
        try:
            summary_dict = json.loads(summary)
        except:
            # 如果解析失败，用简单版本
            summary_dict = {
                "description": summary,
                "difficulty": 3,
                "target_audience": "本科生",
                "prerequisites_detail": "",
                "learning_time": 30,
                "key_concepts": [],
                "common_misconceptions": "",
                "real_world_applications": ""
            }
        
        summaries[topic['name']] = summary_dict
    
    return summaries


# ══════════════════════════════════════════════
# 修改后的主Pipeline
# ══════════════════════════════════════════════

def build_knowledge_graph(document, llm, user_profile=None):
    """
    完整的知识图谱构建Pipeline
    结合你原有方法 + KnowPath的实体摘要
    """
    
    # Step 1+2：你原有的逻辑（不变）
    topics = extract_topics_from_doc(document, llm)
    similarity_edges = build_similarity_edges(topics)
    prerequisite_edges = build_prerequisite_edges(topics, llm)
    
    # Step 3：新增！KnowPath的实体摘要
    print("正在生成实体摘要...")
    summaries = generate_entity_summaries(topics, llm)
    
    # 存入NetworkX图（节点带摘要属性）
    G = nx.DiGraph()
    
    # 添加节点（带摘要）
    for topic in topics:
        name = topic['name']
        summary = summaries.get(name, {})
        
        G.add_node(
            name,
            # 原有属性
            sub_topics=topic.get('sub_topics', []),
            # 新增：KnowPath的摘要属性
            description=summary.get('description', ''),
            difficulty=summary.get('difficulty', 3),
            target_audience=summary.get('target_audience', ''),
            prerequisites_detail=summary.get(
                'prerequisites_detail', ''
            ),
            learning_time=summary.get('learning_time', 30),
            key_concepts=summary.get('key_concepts', []),
            common_misconceptions=summary.get(
                'common_misconceptions', ''
            ),
            real_world_applications=summary.get(
                'real_world_applications', ''
            )
        )
    
    # 添加先修关系边（你原有的）
    for edge in prerequisite_edges:
        G.add_edge(
            edge['from'], edge['to'],
            relation='prerequisite',
            reason=edge.get('reason', '')
        )
    
    # 添加相似关系边（你原有的）
    for edge in similarity_edges:
        G.add_edge(
            edge['a'], edge['b'],
            relation='similarity',
            score=edge.get('similarity', 0)
        )
    
    return G
```

## 五、摘要如何被其他Agent使用

加了摘要之后，你的其他Agent可以直接用：

```python
class PlanningAgent:
    
    def generate_curriculum(self, user_profile, G):
        """
        Planning Agent利用节点摘要
        判断哪些知识点适合这个用户
        """
        
        # 获取用户水平对应的难度范围
        level_to_difficulty = {
            "beginner": (1, 2),
            "undergraduate": (2, 3),
            "postgraduate": (3, 5)
        }
        min_diff, max_diff = level_to_difficulty.get(
            user_profile.level, (1, 5)
        )
        
        # 筛选难度匹配的知识点
        suitable_nodes = []
        for node, attrs in G.nodes(data=True):
            difficulty = attrs.get('difficulty', 3)
            if min_diff <= difficulty <= max_diff:
                suitable_nodes.append({
                    'name': node,
                    'difficulty': difficulty,
                    'learning_time': attrs.get(
                        'learning_time', 30
                    ),
                    'description': attrs.get('description', '')
                })
        
        # 按先修关系排序
        # （你原有的拓扑排序逻辑）
        ordered_nodes = topological_sort(
            suitable_nodes, G
        )
        
        # LLM生成课程表
        prompt = f"""
        根据以下知识点信息生成学习计划：
        
        学生水平：{user_profile.level}
        学习目标：{user_profile.goal}
        可用时间：{user_profile.timeline}
        
        知识点列表（已按先修顺序排列）：
        {json.dumps(ordered_nodes, ensure_ascii=False)}
        
        请生成结构化的学习计划。
        """
        
        return llm.call(prompt)


class ContentAgent:
    
    def generate_personalized_content(
        self, topic_name, G, user_profile
    ):
        """
        Content Agent利用节点摘要
        生成个性化内容
        """
        
        # 获取该知识点的摘要
        node_attrs = G.nodes[topic_name]
        
        prompt = f"""
        请为以下知识点生成个性化学习内容：
        
        知识点：{topic_name}
        知识点描述：{node_attrs.get('description', '')}
        核心概念：{node_attrs.get('key_concepts', [])}
        常见误解：{node_attrs.get('common_misconceptions', '')}
        
        学生信息：
        - 水平：{user_profile.level}
        - 兴趣：{user_profile.interests}
        - 已有知识：{user_profile.prior_knowledge}
        
        要求：
        1. 针对学生水平调整深度
        2. 用学生兴趣领域的例子解释
        3. 特别注意解释常见误解
        """
        
        return llm.call(prompt)
```

## 六、对你论文写作的帮助

加了这一步之后，你可以这样写：

> **"本系统的知识图谱构建方案在传统的实体提取和关系构建基础上，借鉴KnowPath（Zhu等，2026）的实体摘要思想，为每个知识概念节点附加LLM生成的结构化摘要，包含难度等级、适用人群、前置要求等教育元数据。这一设计使Planning Agent能够根据用户画像精准筛选难度匹配的知识点，避免推荐内容过难或过易，同时为Content Agent提供个性化内容生成的上下文依据。"**

## 七、一句话总结

> **你现在的Pipeline已经很好了，只需要在"存入NetworkX"之前加一步"LLM生成节点摘要"，把难度、适用人群、前置要求等信息存进节点属性里。这样知识图谱就从"只有结构"升级为"结构+语义"，其他Agent就能利用这些信息做更精准的个性化决策。**

[06/02/2026 14:23:59 +00:00] MI, YU:
**① 目标驱动的主动学习规划**

**痛点：** 自学者最大的困难不是"这道题不会"，而是"不知道自己该学什么、按什么顺序学、能不能在有限时间内学完"。现有系统无法回答这个问题。

**现有解决方法：** KnowLP、GEHRL 等学习路径推荐系统根据学习者的历史答题记录，推荐"下一道应该练习的题目"。系统是被动响应的，用户需要自己知道自己的目标，然后一步一步跟着走。

**现有方法的文献支持：** KnowLP (Cheng et al., 2025) 在三个公开数据集上取得了 SOTA，证明了知识图谱路径规划的有效性，但其输入是预设的目标知识点集合，不支持自然语言目标输入，也没有时间约束机制。

**我的创新点：** 用户只需用自然语言描述目标和时间（"我想在四周内学懂 Transformer，目前只会基础 Python"），系统全自动生成结构化的周/日学习计划。

**如何实现：** Planning Agent 解析自然语言目标，映射到知识图谱上的目标节点集合；沿先修边回溯，识别学习者尚未掌握的前置知识点，确定最优起始节点；用 BFS/A* 找到路径序列；最后按时间约束分配到每天，生成类似健身 App 训练计划的结构化日历。

**为什么更好：** 这是从推荐系统到规划系统的范式转变。用户不再需要懂"我该学什么"，只需要知道"我想达到什么"，其余全部由系统处理。

---

**② 可解释的 LLM 规划器替代强化学习**

**痛点：** 路径规划的决策过程应该是透明的——学习者有权知道"为什么系统让我先学 A 再学 B"，老师也需要能审查系统的推荐逻辑。但大多数现有方法的决策过程是黑箱。

**现有解决方法：** KnowLP 使用 PPO（近端策略优化）训练强化学习智能体来决定路径，需要大量交互数据和反复训练，决策过程完全不可解释。

**现有方法的文献支持：** KnowLP 的 P-Agent 需要在数据集上训练数千次迭代才能收敛；Yao et al. (2023) 的 ReAct 论文证明，纯推理方法（CoT）幻觉率高达 56%，而引入外部信息锚点后幻觉率降至 0%。

**我的创新点：** 用基于 ReAct 框架的 LLM Planner 替代 PPO，让规划过程完全透明——每一步决策都附带自然语言推理说明，知识图谱作为外部事实锚点控制幻觉。

**如何实现：** Planning Agent 按照"思考→查询知识图谱→观察结果→继续思考"的 ReAct 循环工作，每次查询知识图谱的先修关系或相似关系，推理出下一个应该安排的知识点，并输出带有理由的决策链。

**为什么更好：** 三个优势并存：可解释性（能输出"为什么这样安排"）、零训练成本（不需要历史交互数据）、跨领域泛化（换一个学习领域不需要重新训练）。

---

**③ 知识图谱结构约束下的个性化内容生成**

**痛点：** 生成的学习内容往往是孤立的——讲 Transformer 的时候不会自动铺垫 Attention，讲 Attention 的时候不知道学习者刚学完 CNN。内容和内容之间缺乏知识逻辑上的连贯性。

**现有解决方法：** PAGE (Lim et al., 2025) 提出了个性化内容生成框架，根据学生的背景和兴趣调整讲解方式，效果显著（测验成绩提升约 5%）。但 PAGE 的 RAG 是纯文本检索，不感知知识点之间的依赖结构。

**现有方法的文献支持：** PAGE 证明了 RAG + 学生画像能有效提升学习效果；Abu-Rasheed et al. (2025) 证明了知识图谱能作为 RAG 系统的结构上下文来源，提升内容生成的相关性和准确性。

**我的创新点：** Content Agent 在生成每日讲解时，同时输入三类信息：知识图谱提供的结构上下文（当日知识点的前置节点和相似节点）、RAG 向量库检索到的文档内容、以及学生画像。三者融合后再生成个性化讲解。

**如何实现：** 每次生成内容时，先查询知识图谱获取"这个知识点依赖什么、容易和什么混淆"，将这些结构信息作为额外 prompt 上下文传入 LLM，引导它在讲解时自动建立知识衔接。同时用 PAGE 的开放联网检索机制，根据学生兴趣生成个性化的类比和例子。

**为什么更好：** 内容生成不再只是"和这个话题相关"，而是"在正确的知识依赖顺序下、针对这个学生生成"。知识逻辑连贯性和个性化表达同时兼顾。

---

**④ 三维知识状态驱动的可解释测验选题**

**痛点：** 测验选题如果只看"这个知识点你上次答错了"，会忽略两个重要信息：这道题现在对你来说是否难度合适，以及你有多久没复习这个知识点了。选出来的题要么太难打击积极性，要么太简单没有挑战，要么明明早就忘了却不提醒复习。

**现有解决方法：** 多数系统只用掌握度单一维度选题；KG4EER (Guan et al., 2025) 提出同时考虑掌握度、学习进度和遗忘率三个维度，并为每道推荐题目给出三条可解释理由，在 ASSISTments 数据集上达到 0.936 的准确率。

**现有方法的文献支持：** KG4EER 通过 650 名学生的问卷验证，三类推荐理由的满意度均超过 3.7/5，说明可解释性对学生信任度有实质影响。Ebbinghaus 遗忘曲线是认知心理学的经典理论，为遗忘率的计算提供了理论依据。

**我的创新点：** 将 KG4EER 的三维特征迁移到本系统，但用 LLM 替代 LSTM 模型实现，不需要训练。Quiz Agent 综合掌握度（难度是否匹配）、学习进度（当前阶段是否合适）、遗忘率（是否需要复习）三个维度选题，并为每道题生成自然语言推荐理由。

**如何实现：** 掌握度由每次答题后直接更新；遗忘率用 Ebbinghaus 指数公式实时计算（距上次练习越久，遗忘率越高）；学习进度从当前计划阶段读取；三个值输入 LLM，推理出最合适的题目，并输出"推荐这道题是因为你对 X 的掌握度是 63%，距上次练习已过 5 天"这样的解释。

**为什么更好：** 比单维度选题更精准；比 KG4EER 的原始实现成本更低（无需训练 LSTM 和图嵌入模型）；可解释理由增强学生对系统的信任，有实验数据支撑。

---

**⑤ 包含时间感知和间隔复习的完整自适应闭环**

**痛点：** 学习计划一旦生成就是固定的。学某个知识点卡住了、或者某天没完成任务，计划不会自动调整，要么就此放弃，要么自己手动修改。另外，学过的知识点没有复习机制，很快就忘了。

**现有解决方法：** KnowLP 的 S-Agent 在学习者卡住时切换到相似知识点子路径，是目前最接近动态重规划的工作，但它依赖 RL 训练，且不考虑时间约束，插入子路径后原计划可能超期。

**现有方法的文献支持：** KnowLP 证明了相似关系子路径能有效缓解学习瓶颈，在 step=20 的长路径上优势最显著；间隔重复学习（Spaced Repetition）是记忆科学的核心原则，已被 Duolingo 等产品大规模验证。

**我的创新点：** 在 KnowLP 的相似子路径机制基础上，增加两个新机制：一是插入子路径后自动重新计算剩余时间约束，确保计划不超期；二是当遗忘率超过阈值时，Adaptation Agent 主动在次日计划中插入间隔复习内容。

**如何实现：** 每日测验结束后，Evaluation Agent 更新掌握度向量和遗忘率；若掌握度低于阈值，Adaptation Agent 在 KG 的 similarity 边上查找相似知识点，生成对比学习子路径并插入计划，同时重新分配剩余知识点到剩余天数；若某知识点遗忘率超过 70%，自动在次日插入 10 分钟复习任务。整个调整过程由 Chatbot 向用户透明解释。

**为什么更好：** 计划是活的，不会因为一个瓶颈而崩掉；时间约束始终被尊重，用户的整体目标不会因为动态调整而延期；加入间隔复习后，系统不只是帮助学习，还帮助记忆，形成真正完整的学习闭环。
我的创新点是这样的，我着重1和3这两个，所以第一步是LLM-based KG Construction，我不需要你给我代码，但你能不能给我解释一下文档输入
  ↓
LLM抽取知识点（Topic/SubTopic）
  ↓
KeyBERT + Sentence-BERT → similarity边
  ↓
LLM判断先修关系 → prerequisite边
是不是可以把LLM抽取知识点变成分块（TextTiling自适应分块）

# 一、你的Pipeline可以升级成三步吗？

## 直接对比

```
你现在的Pipeline：
════════════════════════════════════════
文档输入
  ↓
LLM抽取知识点（Topic/SubTopic）
  ↓
KeyBERT + Sentence-BERT → similarity边
  ↓
LLM判断先修关系 → prerequisite边

升级后的三步Pipeline：
════════════════════════════════════════
文档输入
  ↓
Step 1：TextTiling自适应分块
  ↓
Step 2：LLM提取知识点 + 关系
        （包含similarity + prerequisite）
  ↓
Step 3：LLM生成节点摘要
        （难度/适用人群/前置要求）
  ↓
存入NetworkX图
```

## 回答你的问题：可以，但要注意一件事

```
原来：
  LLM抽取知识点 → 然后再分别做相似度和先修关系

升级后：
  Step 1：先分块（TextTiling）
  Step 2：LLM从每个块里提取知识点+关系
          （相似关系 + 先修关系一起提取）
  Step 3：LLM为每个知识点生成摘要

关键区别：
  原来是"先提取所有知识点，再统一算关系"
  升级后是"每个块独立提取，最后合并"
  → 更适合长文档
  → 更符合KnowPath的设计思路
```

---

# 二、KeyBERT + Sentence-BERT是什么？

## 先说Sentence-BERT

```
普通BERT的问题：
  比较两个句子的相似度
  需要把两个句子同时输入
  → 速度慢，不适合大规模比较

Sentence-BERT的解决方案：
  每个句子单独编码成一个向量
  → 句子A → 向量[0.2, 0.8, 0.3, ...]
  → 句子B → 向量[0.3, 0.7, 0.4, ...]
  → 计算两个向量的余弦相似度
  → 快！可以大规模比较

在你的Pipeline里的作用：
  把每个知识点的描述编码成向量
  计算两两之间的余弦相似度
  相似度 > 阈值 → 建立similarity边
```

## 再说KeyBERT

```
问题：
  知识点描述可能很长
  "注意力机制是一种让模型动态关注
   输入序列不同位置的技术，通过计算
   Query、Key、Value的点积..."
  
  直接用整段话计算相似度
  → 噪音太多，不够精准

KeyBERT的作用：
  从长描述中提取最关键的词/短语
  "注意力机制" → 关键词：
    ["Query-Key-Value", "动态权重",
     "序列对齐", "上下文感知"]
  
  用这些关键词代表知识点
  → 相似度计算更精准

两者配合：
  KeyBERT提取关键词
    ↓
  Sentence-BERT把关键词编码成向量
    ↓
  余弦相似度计算
    ↓
  建立similarity边
```

## 通俗比喻

```
Sentence-BERT = 把文字翻译成坐标
  "梯度下降" → 坐标(0.8, 0.3, 0.6)
  "随机梯度下降" → 坐标(0.7, 0.4, 0.5)
  "牛顿法" → 坐标(0.2, 0.9, 0.1)

余弦相似度 = 计算两个坐标的夹角
  夹角小 → 方向相近 → 语义相似
  夹角大 → 方向不同 → 语义不同

KeyBERT = 先提炼精华，再翻译坐标
  避免把无关的词也翻译进去
```

# 四、KG构建之后，接下来怎么实现？

你说着重创新点①和③，所以我给你规划完整的实现顺序：

## 总体实现路线图

```
阶段一（已完成/进行中）：KG构建
  ✅ 文档→分块→提取→摘要→NetworkX图

阶段二：Planning Agent（对应创新点①）
  目标驱动的主动学习规划

阶段三：Content Agent（对应创新点③）
  知识图谱结构约束下的个性化内容生成

阶段四：Quiz + Evaluation Agent（对应创新点④）
  三维知识状态驱动的测验选题

阶段五：Adaptation Agent（对应创新点⑤）
  时间感知的自适应闭环

阶段六：前端集成
  把所有Agent串联成完整系统
```

---

## 阶段二：Planning Agent（创新点①）

### 核心任务

```
输入：
  "我想在四周内学懂Transformer，
   目前只会基础Python"

输出：
  结构化的28天学习日历
  每天：主题 + 预计时间 + 学习目标
```

### 实现步骤

```
Step 1：解析自然语言目标
  LLM把"学懂Transformer"
  → 映射到KG上的目标节点
  → [Transformer, Self-Attention, 
     Multi-Head Attention, ...]

Step 2：识别起始节点
  查询用户已有知识（"只会基础Python"）
  → 在KG上标记已掌握节点
  → 沿先修边回溯
  → 找到最远的未掌握前置节点
  → 这就是起始点

Step 3：BFS/A*路径搜索
  从起始节点到目标节点
  沿先修关系边搜索
  → 得到有序知识点序列

Step 4：时间约束分配
  每个节点有learning_time属性（摘要里有！）
  按时间约束分配到每天
  → 生成日历结构
```

### ReAct循环（对应创新点②）

```
Thought：用户想学Transformer，
         我需要先找到KG上的目标节点
Action：查询KG（search_node("Transformer")）
Obs：找到节点，先修节点为[Self-Attention, 
     Positional Encoding, ...]
Thought：用户只会Python，
         需要回溯找起始节点
Action：查询KG（get_prerequisites_chain(...)）
Obs：最远前置节点是"线性代数基础"
Thought：现在用BFS找路径
Action：BFS搜索
Obs：路径序列[线性代数→...→Transformer]
Thought：按28天分配时间
Action：调用时间分配函数
Finish：输出28天课程表
```

---

## 阶段三：Content Agent（创新点③）

### 核心任务

```
输入：
  今日主题（如"Attention Mechanism"）
  + KG结构上下文
  + RAG检索内容
  + 学生画像

输出：
  个性化讲义
  （知识逻辑连贯 + 符合学生兴趣）
```

### 实现步骤

```
Step 1：查询KG获取结构上下文
  今日主题：Attention Mechanism
  → 查先修节点：[Softmax, Dot Product]
    "学生刚学完这些"
  → 查相似节点：[Self-Attention, Memory Network]
    "这些容易混淆"
  → 查节点摘要：难度3，常见误解是...

Step 2：RAG检索相关文档
  从向量数据库检索Attention相关内容
  （d2l.ai的原文）

Step 3：开放联网搜索（PAGE的方法）
  根据学生兴趣生成搜索词
  学生喜欢游戏 → 
  "attention mechanism in game AI"

Step 4：三路融合生成
  KG结构上下文 + RAG内容 + 联网内容
  → 统一输入LLM
  → 生成个性化讲义

关键Prompt设计：
  "今天学Attention，
   学生刚学完Softmax和点积（先修）
   → 要建立知识衔接
   容易和Self-Attention混淆（相似）
   → 要主动区分
   学生喜欢游戏
   → 用游戏AI的例子解释"
```

---

## 阶段四：Quiz + Evaluation Agent（创新点④）

```
Quiz Agent：
  三维选题
  掌握度（每次答题后更新）
  + 遗忘率（Ebbinghaus公式实时计算）
  + 学习进度（当前阶段）
  → LLM推理选题 + 生成解释

Evaluation Agent：
  计算EP = (score_after - score_before)
           / (100 - score_before)
  更新User Profile
  标记薄弱概念
```

---

## 阶段五：Adaptation Agent（创新点⑤）

```
触发条件1：掌握度 < 70%
  → 查KG similarity边
  → 找相似知识点
  → 生成对比学习子路径
  → 重新计算剩余时间分配

触发条件2：遗忘率 > 70%
  → 在次日计划插入10分钟复习
  → Chatbot向用户解释原因
```

```
Onboarding → Planning → 每日循环
（自然语言输入）  （课程日历）  （内容+测验+适应）
```

给我一段写在ppt上的kg构建的英文描述

Response:
# LLM-Based Knowledge Graph Construction

---

## Option 1：简洁版（适合标题页或概述）

**LLM-Based Knowledge Graph Construction**

> This system constructs a domain knowledge graph from raw educational documents through a three-stage pipeline. First, input documents are segmented into semantically coherent chunks using the TextTiling algorithm. Second, GPT-4o extracts knowledge concepts (Topics and Sub-Topics) along with their prerequisite relationships from each chunk, while KeyBERT and Sentence-BERT compute semantic similarity scores to establish similarity edges between conceptually related nodes. Third, GPT-4o generates structured node summaries for each concept, capturing difficulty level, target audience, and prerequisite requirements. The resulting graph is stored in NetworkX with two distinct edge types — prerequisite edges encoding learning dependencies and similarity edges encoding conceptual relatedness — providing the structural backbone for downstream Planning and Content Agents.

---

## Option 2：分点版（适合正文slide，每点对应一个bullet）

**LLM-Based Knowledge Graph Construction**

**Stage 1 — Adaptive Document Chunking**
> Input documents are segmented using the TextTiling algorithm with a dynamic upper bound of 800 tokens per chunk, preserving semantic boundaries rather than enforcing fixed-length splits.

**Stage 2 — Entity and Relation Extraction**
> GPT-4o extracts knowledge concepts (Topics and Sub-Topics) from each chunk and identifies prerequisite relationships through structured prompting. Semantic similarity edges are computed independently using KeyBERT for keyword extraction and Sentence-BERT for cosine similarity scoring, with edges created above a threshold of 0.75.

**Stage 3 — Node Summary Generation**
> GPT-4o generates a structured metadata summary for each concept node, including difficulty level (1–5), target audience, prerequisite details, estimated learning time, and key sub-concepts. These summaries enable the Planning Agent to filter concepts by learner level and provide the Content Agent with contextual grounding for personalised content generation.

**Output**
> A directed NetworkX graph with two edge types: prerequisite edges (→) encoding sequential learning dependencies, and similarity edges (- -) encoding conceptual relatedness, serving as the structural backbone of the adaptive learning system.

---

## Option 3：一句话版（适合放在系统架构图旁边的注释）

> The knowledge graph is constructed via a three-stage LLM pipeline: (1) TextTiling-based adaptive chunking, (2) GPT-4o entity and relation extraction augmented by KeyBERT + Sentence-BERT similarity scoring, and (3) GPT-4o node summary generation capturing difficulty, prerequisites, and learning time — producing a structured graph that grounds both path planning and personalised content generation.

[06/09/2026 04:55:26 +00:00] MI, YU:
Gateway Assessment Report

English Version

Title: An LLM-Based Multi-Agent System for Personalised Autonomous Learning
Student: [Your Name] | Supervisor: [Supervisor Name] | Date: June 2025

1. Context and Literature Review
1.1 Background
The proliferation of online learning platforms has made educational content more accessible than ever, yet self-directed learners continue to face a fundamental challenge that technology has largely failed to address: not knowing what to study, in what order, or whether a learning goal is achievable within a given timeframe. Unlike supervised classroom environments, autonomous learners must simultaneously act as both student and curriculum designer — a dual burden that frequently leads to inefficient study patterns, abandoned learning goals, and fragmented knowledge acquisition. Existing intelligent tutoring systems and learning path recommendation platforms have made progress in personalising content delivery, but they remain fundamentally reactive: they respond to what a learner has already done rather than proactively planning what a learner should do next. This project addresses that gap by proposing a system that treats learning as a planning problem rather than a recommendation problem.
1.2 Existing Approaches and Their Limitations
Research in intelligent educational systems has converged around three primary technical paradigms, each with distinct strengths and limitations.
The first paradigm is reinforcement learning-based path recommendation. KnowLP (Cheng et al., 2025), the current state-of-the-art on three public benchmarks, constructs a dual knowledge graph encoding both prerequisite and similarity relations between concepts, then trains a PPO-based agent to navigate this graph and recommend learning sequences. GEHRL (Li et al., CIKM 2023) extends this approach with hierarchical reinforcement learning, introducing a high-level agent for goal planning and a low-level agent for item recommendation. While these systems demonstrate strong empirical performance, they share two critical limitations. First, both require substantial historical interaction data for training, making them impractical for cold-start scenarios or novel learning domains. Second, and more fundamentally, their goal inputs are structured node sets — predefined knowledge concept identifiers — rather than natural language expressions. A learner who wishes to "understand Transformer architecture in four weeks" cannot interact with these systems without first manually identifying the corresponding graph nodes, a requirement that defeats the purpose of an intelligent assistant. Furthermore, the PPO-based decision process is entirely opaque: learners have no way of understanding why the system recommends a particular sequence.
The second paradigm is LLM-based path planning without structural constraints. PLPP (Chee Ng et al., 2024) demonstrates that prompt engineering alone can drive GPT-4 to generate personalised learning paths, achieving an 88.3% accuracy rate and significant improvements in long-term knowledge retention compared to baseline methods. This approach eliminates the need for training data and supports natural language goal input. However, because it relies entirely on the LLM's parametric knowledge without grounding in an external knowledge structure, the generated paths can be inconsistent across runs and cannot guarantee that prerequisite ordering is respected. The system also lacks any mechanism for time-constrained planning.
The third paradigm is personalised content generation. PAGE (Lim et al., 2025) proposes a RAG-based framework that retrieves relevant knowledge chunks and adapts their presentation to individual student backgrounds and interests, producing measurable improvements in quiz performance of approximately 5%. EduPlanner (Zhang et al., IEEE TLT 2025) introduces an adversarial multi-agent loop — comprising an evaluator agent, an optimizer agent, and an analyst agent — that iteratively refines lesson plan quality, achieving a CIDDP score of 88 compared to 49 for direct GPT-4 generation. KA-RAG (Gao et al., Applied Sciences 2025) combines a structured course knowledge graph with agentic RAG for educational question answering, achieving 91.4% retrieval accuracy. However, PAGE's retrieval pipeline is purely vector-based and does not model the dependency structure between knowledge concepts, meaning that generated content lacks cross-session coherence. EduPlanner targets teachers producing lesson documents rather than learners engaged in sequential study. KA-RAG addresses single-turn question answering without learning path awareness or sequential content generation.
1.3 Research Gap
No existing system simultaneously addresses all of the following: natural language learning goal input with time constraints, knowledge graph-grounded path planning without training data requirements, structurally coherent sequential content generation across a multi-week learning programme, and transparent explainable decision-making throughout. This project is designed to fill that gap.

2. Aims and Objectives
2.1 Research Aim
This project aims to design, implement, and evaluate an LLM-based multi-agent system that enables self-directed learners to specify learning goals in natural language and receive a complete, adaptive, and explainable personalised learning experience — encompassing structured curriculum planning, knowledge-graph-grounded content generation, adaptive assessment, and dynamic plan adjustment — without requiring any model training or pre-existing interaction data.
2.2 Specific Objectives and Innovations
Innovation 1: Goal-Driven Active Learning Planning
The system's Planning Agent accepts natural language goal descriptions (e.g., "I want to understand Transformer architecture in four weeks, starting from basic Python") and automatically generates a structured day-by-day learning calendar. This is achieved through a three-stage pipeline: the LLM parses the natural language input and maps it to target nodes in the knowledge graph; a BFS/A* traversal of prerequisite edges identifies the optimal learning path from the learner's current knowledge state to the target; and a time allocation algorithm distributes concepts across available days, weighted by the learner's Skill-Tree profile. This represents a paradigm shift from reactive recommendation — where the system responds to what a learner has done — to proactive planning, where the system determines what a learner should do to achieve a specified goal within a specified timeframe.
Innovation 2: Explainable LLM Planner as Replacement for Reinforcement Learning
Rather than training a PPO agent as in KnowLP, the Planning Agent operates via the ReAct framework (Yao et al., ICLR 2023), which interleaves natural language reasoning with knowledge graph queries in a Thought → Action → Observation cycle. Every scheduling decision is accompanied by a natural language justification (e.g., "Linear Algebra is scheduled before Gradient Descent because matrix operations are the mathematical foundation of the attention mechanism"). This design achieves three simultaneous advantages: full explainability of the planning process, zero training cost, and cross-domain generalisation without retraining. The ReAct framework has been empirically shown to reduce hallucination rates from 56% (chain-of-thought reasoning alone) to 0% by grounding decisions in external factual anchors — in this case, the knowledge graph.
Innovation 3: Knowledge Graph-Constrained Personalised Content Generation
The Content Agent generates each day's lecture by fusing three information streams: structural context retrieved from the knowledge graph (prerequisite nodes the learner has recently studied, and similarity nodes representing easily confused concepts), relevant document chunks retrieved via RAG from the course knowledge base, and the learner's cognitive-affective profile (a Skill-Tree capturing domain-specific ability dimensions, combined with interest and learning style preferences drawn from PAGE). This three-way fusion is injected into the system prompt, directing the LLM to build explicit knowledge bridges from previously studied material and proactively disambiguate easily confused concepts. An evaluation-optimisation loop adapted from EduPlanner's adversarial collaboration mechanism iteratively refines generated content against a six-dimensional quality rubric (CIDDP plus a KG Coherence dimension added by this system), with a maximum of three refinement rounds. This ensures that content is not merely topically relevant but knowledge-logically coherent across the entire learning sequence.

3. Plan to Complete the Project
3.1 System Architecture
The system comprises five specialised LLM agents operating within a shared infrastructure of a knowledge graph (NetworkX/Neo4j), a vector database (ChromaDB), and a user profile store. The knowledge graph is constructed offline from educational documents through a three-stage pipeline: adaptive semantic chunking using the TextTiling algorithm with an 800-token upper bound; entity and relation extraction via GPT-4o-mini structured prompting combined with Sentence-BERT cosine similarity for semantic edges; and structured node summary generation encoding difficulty level, estimated learning time, prerequisite dependencies, and common misconceptions. The five agents — Planning, Content, Quiz, Evaluation, and Adaptation — communicate through a shared orchestration layer and maintain state via the user profile, which is updated after every quiz session to reflect current mastery scores and forgetting rates computed via the Ebbinghaus exponential decay formula.
3.2 Project Timeline
Month 1 — Infrastructure (June 9 – July 9)
Week 1–2 focuses on completing the knowledge graph construction pipeline and validating it against a machine learning textbook dataset. Week 3 implements the Planning Agent, targeting the ability to accept a natural language goal and return a structured 30-day curriculum. Week 4 implements the baseline Content Agent with KG context injection.
Month 2 — Integration (July 9 – August 9)
Weeks 5–6 implement the Quiz Agent (drawing on KG4EER's three-dimensional feature framework for exercise selection), the Evaluation Agent (mastery score updates and forgetting rate tracking), and the Adaptation Agent (similarity-relation-based remedial content insertion). Week 7 conducts end-to-end integration testing across a complete simulated seven-day learning session. Week 8 finalises the experimental design, including ablation study configurations and user study recruitment.
Month 3 — Evaluation and Writing (August 9 – September 9)
Weeks 9–10 execute the ablation experiments and user study. Weeks 11–12 are dedicated to thesis writing and revision.
3.3 Evaluation Strategy
The system will be evaluated across four dimensions, each grounded in existing literature to enable meaningful comparison.
Content Quality will be measured using the CIDDP framework (Clarity, Integrity, Depth, Practicality, Pertinence) augmented with a KG Coherence dimension introduced by this work. An LLM-as-Judge protocol will score generated content on a 0–100 scale. The primary comparison will be an ablation study contrasting three conditions: pure RAG generation (Approach A), RAG with student profile (Approach B, replicating PAGE), and the full system with KG context and optimisation loop (Approach C). The benchmark reference is EduPlanner's reported score of 88 for the full system versus 49 for direct GPT-4 generation.
Learning Effectiveness will be measured through pre- and post-quiz score comparisons across a seven-day learning session. The baseline condition is Approach A (pure RAG), and the target improvement threshold is drawn from PAGE's reported 5–6% quiz score improvement attributable to personalisation.
Path Quality will be quantified using the E_P metric adopted from KnowLP, defined as the normalised improvement in goal concept mastery from session start to end. Comparison conditions include random concept ordering and the system-generated path.
Explainability and User Satisfaction will be assessed via a structured questionnaire administered to 5–10 participants following a complete learning session. The benchmark is KG4EER's reported satisfaction score of 3.7–3.83 out of 5 across three recommendation explanation dimensions.
